### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
煤价格分别上涨 2.34%、2.69%；建材相关价格边际回升，熟料、全国水
泥均价和石油沥青价格环比分别上涨 7.90%、3.17%、9.09%，PVC 价格
上涨 3.09%，螺纹钢现货价格小幅上涨 0.36%。有色金属表现分化，铜价
环比上涨 0.27%，铝价下降 2.50%；新能源相关商品仍偏弱，碳酸锂价格
环比下降 22.33%，纯碱、光伏级多晶硅和热轧卷价格分别下降 2.92%、
0.60%、1.98%。终端食品价格整体偏弱，农产品批发价格 200 指数环比
下降 2.96%，猪肉批发价下降 3.64%，蔬菜和鸡蛋价格分别下降 8.53%、
12.57%；汽油、柴油市场价分别上涨 0.54%、5.08%。 
生产端结构分化，需求端仍受内需偏弱与假期扰动共同影响 。10 月，生
产端结构分化，长流程钢铁和部分化工行业偏弱，短流程钢铁、水泥发运
等指标边际改善；需求端受国庆假期影响较大，电影票房明显走强， 汽车
零售、快递和公路货运环比回落；地产销售同比继续走弱、二手房价格下
行，指向地产修复基础仍不牢固。价格端看，能源、化工和建材价格回
升，农产品价格继续下降，显示当前价格修复更多由上游工业品推动，尚
未形成向终端需求和消费价格的广泛传导。往后看，需重点观察假期扰动
消退后生产和物流等能否恢复，以及上游价格上涨能否在需求改善的配合
下形成更持续的价格修复。 
风险提示：国内稳增长政策推进不及预期， 地产市场修复不及预期，海外
需求超预期回落，能源及大宗商品价格波动超预期，地缘政治冲突加剧。

1. 生产端 
1.1 开工率 
截至 10 月第四周， 高炉开工率 （247 家） 为80.01%， 环比下降0.49 个百分点； 电
炉开工率为 64.10%，环比下降 0.99 个百分点；冷轧开工率为 83.02%，环比持平；螺
纹钢开工率为 35.73%， 环比下降2.75 个百分点； 磨机运转率为39.92%， 环比下降2.28
个百分点；浮法玻璃开工率为 66.75%，环比持平；石油沥青装置开工率为 23.90%，环
比上升 5.75 个百分点；PVC 开工率为 61.47%，环比下降 0.63 个百分点；纯碱开工率
为 66.55%，环比上升 2.89 个百分点；PTA 开工率为 69.01%，环比上升 2.04 个百分
点；全钢胎开工率为 57.79%，环比上升 0.16 个百分点；半钢胎开工率为 64.78%，环
比持平。

1.2 库存 
库存方面， 螺纹钢库存为147.21 万吨， 环比下降3.39%； 冷轧库存为129.50 万吨，
环比下降 0.99%；热卷库存为 360.40 万吨，环比下降 2.24%；铁矿石港口库存为
16,462.55 万吨，环比上升 0.14%；焦煤库存（247 家钢厂）为 761.14 万吨，环比下降
0.42%； 煤炭重点电厂库存可用天数为25.20 天， 环比增加9.57%； 水泥库容比为59.22%，
环比下降 0.22 个百分点； 水泥发运率为41.32%， 环比下降4.20 个百分点； 浮法玻璃库
存为 7,051.90 万重量箱，环比下降 0.72%；纯碱企业库存为 136.71 万吨，环比下降
7.88%；沥青仓库库存为 31,870 吨，环比持平。

量为 182.60 万吨，环比上升 0.33%；螺纹钢产量为 181.22 万吨，环比上升 3.68%；热
轧卷板产量为 288.15 万吨，环比上升 0.67%；冷轧卷板产量为 85.03 万吨，环比下降
0.07%； 水泥产量为14,400.60 万吨， 环比上升11.56%； 石油沥青产量为240.90 万吨，

2. 需求端 
2.1 地产 
截至 10 月第四周， 地产方面，30 城商品房成交面积为 172.90 万平方米， 环比增长
10.21%；100 城土地成交占地面积为 1,917.75 万平方米， 环比下降51.44%； 成交土地
溢价率为 1.39%，环比上升 0.72 个百分点。二手房挂牌量指数全国为 0.70，环比下降
6.64%；一线城市为 0.07，环比持平；二线城市为 0.44，环比下降 2.22%；三线城市为
1.01，环比下降 9.82%。二手房挂牌价指数全国为 139.72，环比上涨 0.04%； 一线城市
为 170.34， 环比下降0.27%； 二线城市为126.09， 环比上涨0.09%； 三线城市为120.72，
环比下降 0.01%。

### Page 22

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对均值基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对均值基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对均值基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对均值基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对均值基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に

2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し
～転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方を探る～
（2026年10月2日発刊）
調査部レポートのご紹介 PR
2026年10月作成
レポート
URL
レポート
二次元
コード
世界経済はどこへ向かうのか？中東危機やAI・レジリエンス関連の投資が
もたらす変化や、経済・金融市場の行方を展望
【ポイント①】
中東危機が⾧期化するなかでも、AI・レジリエンス関連の旺盛な投資需要に
支えられ、世界経済は3％台の成⾧ペースを維持
【ポイント②】
世界はインフレ・高金利の新常態へ。成⾧・負担の偏在が経済主体間の
「耐性格差」を拡大
【ポイント③】

### Page 1

M
Update
Nuvoton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Losses Widen; BMC Growth and
Japan Restructuring Remain Key
3Q losses widened Q/Q amid lower gross profit, increased opex
and reduced non-operating income. Management sees scope for
faster BMC growth in 2027, with execution and Japan
restructuring critical to delivering earnings growth.
3Q losses widened: Revenue was NT$7.86bn, -1% Q/Q, +8% Y/Y, 3% above our
estimate; gross profit was NT$2.9bn, -3% Q/Q, +13% Y/Y, 6% above our forecast;

gross margin was 37.4%, -0.6ppt Q/Q, +1.7ppt Y/Y, 1.2ppt above our estimate. Loss
per share was NT$1.24, wider Q/Q, Y/Y, and vs. our expectation. Management
attributed gross margin weakness to product mix; back-end supply constraints and
substantial AI-related R&D left operational progress around 1Q behind plan.
Demand remains mixed: Auto demand has stabilized after prior Y/Y declines, but
management sees no clear rebound. PC demand was significantly weaker in 2H26

than in 1H26 with higher content per PC vs. 2025 helping cushion the impact of
lower volumes. Memory cost increases have been passed through on memory-
bundled MPUs, although some customers began pulling back in 4Q26. MCU
repricing reflects foundry and OSAT costs. Most 2027 capacity has been secured but
cost pass-through and supply availability do not establish margin expansion.
BMC and Japan remain the key milestones: Management sees scope for 2027 BMC

Y/Y growth to exceed 2026's, with existing customers and multiple new customers,
including a CSP, contributing. New customer revenue should initially be small with
fuller contributions over one to two years. Current-gen products should dominate
2027 shipments; next-gen volumes may start modestly in 2H27. Japan remains the
source of losses, making the Tower JV restructuring targeted by management for
end-March 2027 central to recovery.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Ethan Jia

Nuvoton: Valuation Methodology
PT is unchanged at NT$95.00: We factor in our earnings estimate changes. We maintain
the other assumptions in our RI model, including a 9.2% cost of equity (beta of 1.2, risk-
free rate of 2.0% and risk premium of 6.0%), payout ratio of 55%, a medium-term growth
rate of 7.0% and a terminal growth rate of 3.5%. Our bull- and bear-case scenario values
also are unchanged at NT$143.00 and NT$48.00,  respectively.

Risk Reward - Nuvoton Technology Corporation (4919.TW)
NT$95.00
Base case, residual income model. We assume a 9.2% cost of equity (beta 1.2, risk-free rate
2.0% and risk premium 6.0%), a payout ratio of 55%, a medium-term growth rate of 7.0%,
and a terminal growth rate of 3.5%.
▪ Demand in the auto and industrial
segments is recovering, but at a mild pace.
▪ Nuvoton is well positioned to expand MPU
share through bundled sales with Winbond's

DDR4.
▪ We also see business opportunities in
BMC/BMIC for AI servers.
▪ We expect the company to remain loss-
making in 4Q26 and are awaiting GM
recovery before turning more positive on
the stock, which we view as overvalued.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

M
Idea
3Q preview: Co-Tech is scheduled to report its 3Q results on November 6. We
estimate 3Q revenue at NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y), primarily driven by
higher copper prices and product-mix shifts. We lower our 3Q GM estimate from
28.2% to 27.0% (-30bps q/q, +650bps y/y), reflecting the impact of higher
production costs and copper prices during the quarter. Co-Tech has already reported
preliminary monthly profits for August and September at the request of the TWSE

(report link); combined August and September net profit now accounts for
approximately 67% of our 3Q estimate. We estimate 3Q net profit at NT$610mn
(+12% q/q, +141% y/y), implying EPS of NT$2.41.
4Q GM should improve sequentially: We expect 4Q revenue to decline slightly q/q
to NT$3,074mn (-3% q/q, +42% y/y), primarily due to the end of production for low-
end HTE copper foil and the lack of new capacity coming online. However, we expect
Co-Tech’s GM to expand 700bps q/q to 34%, driven by an improved product mix, a

~10% processing-fee increase effective from 4Q onward, and lower electricity rates
compared with 2Q–3Q, when summer electricity rates were in effect. We forecast
4Q EPS of NT$3.02 (+25% q/q, +102% y/y). For CY26, we estimate revenue will
increase approximately 47% y/y to NT$11.6bn, with GM expanding 760bps y/y to
29.3%, implying EPS of NT$9.62 (+129% y/y).
Potential processing-fee hike: Our supply-chain checks suggest that a key
competitor may announce processing-fee increases as early as December. If so, we

expect Co-Tech to follow with a price increase as well.
Upward revisions to our capacity assumptions ( Exhibit 3 ): Co-Tech has
announced plans to expand its greenfield capacity in Malaysia (report link). The
Malaysian expansion will be implemented in two phases, adding approximately 20k
tons of annual HVLP3/4 capacity from late 2028 onward. RG-series capacity is also
expected to increase modestly through the conversion of older HTE production
lines.

Raised PT to NT$745; stay OW: The increase in our price target is driven by our
earnings estimate changes for CY26-28. Our new price target implies 21x CY28 P/E
and the stock currently trades at only 12x CY28 P/E. We also find the valuation
attractive compared to  its past 3Y P/E average of ~20x.
What’s Changed
Co-Tech Development Corp. (8358.TWO) From To
Price Target NT$730.00 NT$745.00

3Q preliminary revenue came in above our estimate, but we
lower our 3Q GM forecast to factor in the higher production
costs and copper pricing. 4Q GM should improve meaningfully
sequentially. We raise our price target and stay OW.

We update our HVLP4 copper foil supply-demand model to factor in the changes in our
capacity assumptions for Co-Tech, as well as the latest expansion plans announced by
Chinese and Korean suppliers, which leads to a ~8% undersupply in 2027 followed by a

~13% supply surplus in 2028.

Price target and valuation methodology
Our price target for Co-Tech increases  ~2% to NT$745: This implies 39x CY27 or 21x
CY28 P/E. Our price target is also our base case scenario value. It is derived from our

residual income (RI) model (similar to the rest of our tech hardware universe).
To calculate the fair value of the stock, we use the company's beginning equity plus the
present value of all expected residual income of earnings in excess of cost of capital. The
RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and negative when it is below the
cost of capital. Our key assumptions include:
•  A cost of equity of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7%, and risk-free rate

of 1.0%),
• a medium-term growth rate of 11.5% (lowered from 13% previously, as we enter a
higher base in CY27),
• a terminal growth rate of 3%.
The 21x CY28 P/E implied by our price target is above its  past 3Y average  of 20x. We deem
this valuation as fair, because Co-Tech is benefitting from an earnings growth cycle fueled
by stronger AI server/networking CCL demand, Co-Tech's continued mix improvement
(from HTE + RTF (RG) copper foils to advanced RTF (RG) + HVLP3/4 copper foils) and

continued  processing fee adjustments, which supports margin expansion. The stock is
trading at just 12x CY28 P/E, below Co-Tech's past 3Y average of 20x. Thus, we view the
risk reward as attractive.
Our bull- and bear-case values also increase ~2% to NT$1,225 and NT$305,
respectively.

MS Rating
▪ Co-Tech has exited the low-margin HTE
copper foil business, which should support
proﬁtability improvement.
▪ Co-Tech has a dominant share in the
server R-PCB copper foil market with its RG
copper foils (equivalent to HVLP1/2), making
it a key beneﬁciary of global data center
infrastructure build-outs.
▪ Co-Tech is proactively expanding its
HVLP4 capacity to support key AI
customers' high-end copper foil demand. It
should have a 22% supply share by 2028, on

our estimates.
▪ Risk-reward is attractive with the stock
trading at 12x CY28 P/E vs the 3Y historical
average of 20x.

NT$1,225.00
35x 2028e P/E
We assume high-end servers and switches
see signiﬁcantly stronger growth in 2027-28,

driven by faster-than-expected new data
center build-outs and smooth ramp of new
generations of AI/networking chips; we also
assume copper foil demand from key
satellite customer will be stronger than
expected. We assume Co-Tech's copper foil
demand from the mentioned applications
remains robust while its product mix sees
continued improvement from the adoption
of HVLP4+ copper foil in more applications.
NT$745.00
21x 2028e P/E
Co-Tech continues to grow materially driven

by AI server demand growth, material grade
upgrades and better pricing power due to
scarce high-end capacity and continued shift
toward higher-end applications.
NT$305.00
9x 2028e P/E
We assume high-end servers and switches
see signiﬁcantly weaker demand in 2027-28,
driven by slowdown in new data center
build-outs and delayed ramp of new
generations of AI/networking chips; we also
assume copper foil demand from key
satellite customer will be stagnant. As a

result, we assume Co-Tech's copper foil
demand from the aforementioned
applications becomes sluggish and its
customers' migration to the HVLP4+
platform to be put on hold.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 3

NYPCB (8046.TW)
05 November 2026 Citi Research
3
NYPCB
Valuation
Our DCF-based TP for NYPCB is NT$1,700. With a risk-free rate of 5.00% (10-year government bond rate), a market risk
premium of 7% and an equity beta of 1.22 (YTD weekly share price), we calculate NYPCB's WACC as 13.4%. Our TP is equivalent
to 30x/13x 2027E EPS/BVPS, which reflects its high P/E multiple during upcycles. NYPCB traded at 15-20x P/E during its
2003-2008 upcycle driven by PC/NB demand strength. In 2010-11 when the market was recovering from the global financial

crisis, NYPCB traded at 25-35x P/E. In the last 2019-22 upcycle when ABF was in shortage, NYPCB traded at 30-40x P/E.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the NYPCB shares from reaching our target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected ABF
demand from key customers; 2) BT business seasonality weakness and fewer-than-expected price hikes; and 3) severe T glass
shortages that cause a bottleneck to its production.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

晶豪科 (3006 TT)  Trading Buy

報價收斂，後續成長動能受限，建議 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ：AI 伺服器對 HBM 多層晶圓堆疊的強勁需求大幅吸
納三大原廠先進 DRAM 產能 ， 引發傳統記憶體供給端嚴重缺口 ，
缺貨潮由 DDR5 與 DDR4 蔓延至 DDR3 、 DDR2 及 NOR/NAND 
Flash 。同業大廠如華邦電 (2344 TT) 基於晶圓廠經濟效益考量，
產能優先配置予大容量 DDR4 產品，對 DDR2 與 DDR3 採取停
供或減供措施，大幅限縮市場流通供給。目前晶豪科 DDR2 與
DDR3 合計佔 DRAM 營收比重超過 50% ，在同業減供退出的趨
勢下，晶豪科已成為市場核心主要供應商，市占地位結構性擴
張。預估晶豪科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.72 元。雖然記憶體價格
在 2027 年仍可以維持高檔，但目前報價漲幅開始收斂，產能供
給受限，成本持續上揚，後續成長動能受限，因此對晶豪科投
資建議為 Trading Buy 。  
3Q26 稅後 EPS 36.09 元： 受惠國際大廠把產能轉至 HBM、DDR5
等高階產品時，成熟型 DRAM、DDR2/DDR3 等利基型記憶體供
給減少，帶動報價上揚，晶豪科 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元，
QoQ+58.83% ，毛利率增加 8.75 個百分點至 77.44% ，3Q26 稅後
淨利 107.59 億元，QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元。1Q26~3Q26
營收 433.63 億元，年增 3.439 倍，毛利率 69.62% ，較 2025 年
同期增 60.08 個百分點，歸屬母公司淨利 187.3 億元，稅後 EPS 
64.54 元。  
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 39.41 元： 展望 4Q26 營運表現，儘管整體
出貨數量受限於上游晶圓代工與封測端產能配額難見大幅擴
增，但由於 4Q26 產品平均銷售單價 (ASP) 延續上漲趨勢，漲幅
約 10~20% 。但晶圓代工從 07/2026 漲約 45% ，封測約漲 20% ，
將會逐步反映在 4Q26 銷貨成本，經營團隊預期 4Q26 毛利率將
穩健維持在 3Q26 之歷史高檔區間，來自產品售價調升幅度足
以抵銷成本增幅。預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 39.41 元。預估 2026 年
稅後 EPS 102.25 元。

2 群益投顧 
  晶豪科 ESG ：  
企業永續與治理架構  
• 企業永續委員會：於 10/2020 成立，負責評估永續經營的風險與機會、制
訂永續發展願景與策略。  
• 核心推動構面：涵蓋強化公司治理、夥伴關係、環境永續、友善職場及社
會公益。  
• 永續報告書：定期依循國際標準 (如  GRI 、 TCFD 、 SASB 等 )編製與揭露
年度永續報告書 ， 可至晶豪科技 ESG 報告書或耀智永續報告書資料庫  查閱
與下載。  
環境與氣候行動  
• 減碳目標：積極因應全球氣候變遷，設定具體減碳目標，其減碳表現經評
估朝向升溫  1.5°C 以下的目標邁進 (實測減碳溫度約  1.506°C) 。  
• 永續金融連結：曾與金融機構 (如台新銀行、北富銀等 )合作推動永續績效
聯貸案 (30 億元聯貸案 )，將  ESG 指標與授信條件結合，落實綠色金融與企
業社會責任。  
社會責任與人才培育  
• 社會公益與教育：設立「晶豪科技教育基金會」，長期投入教育與社會公
益相關推廣。  
• 人才與實習計畫：積極與青年世代接軌，辦理暑期研發與  ESG 實習生計
畫，深化校園人才交流與職場體驗。  
記憶體價格調漲，晶豪科 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元， QoQ+58.83% ，稅後淨利
107.59 億元， QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元：  
晶豪科為國內利基型記憶體  IC 設計公司，產品涵蓋利基型 DRAM 產品組
合，涵蓋從傳統 SDRAM 到主流的 DDR 、 DDR2 、 DDR3 及 DDR4 。廣泛應
用於消費性電子、工業控制、網通設備、 PC 週邊，公司主要是 4Gb 以下為
主。在 Flash 方面，晶豪科有 SLC NAND Flash 以及編碼型快閃記憶體 (NOR 
Flash) 。因應中低階智慧手機、系統級封裝 (SiP) 及固態硬碟 (SSD) 需求快速
成長，公司以 MCP/eMCP/eMMC 為主力產品。類比和數位的產品
(Audio/Power/Motor Driver/Sensor) 佔公司營收比重低。各產品佔比， DRAM
佔營收比重最高，約 60~65% ， Flash 和其他約佔 30~33%(MCP 等也有含
DRAM) ，非記憶體佔營收比重 5%。  
受惠國際大廠把產能轉至 HBM 、 DDR5 等高階產品時，成熟型 DRAM 、
DDR2/DDR3 等利基型記憶體供給減少，帶動報價上揚，晶豪科 3Q26 營收
221.50 億元， QoQ+58.83% ，毛利率增加 8.75 個百分點至 77.44% ， 3Q26 稅
後淨利 107.59 億元， QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元。  
累計 1Q26~3Q26 營收 433.63 億元，年增 3.439 倍，毛利率 69.62% ，較 2025
年同期增 60.08 個百分點，歸屬母公司淨利 187.3 億元，稅後 EPS 64.54 元。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 3 日

3 群益投顧 
3Q26 底晶豪科的存貨為 90.76 億元，庫存水準較 2Q26 的 79.56 億元，
QoQ+14.08% ，要反映晶圓廠與封測調價造成的成本墊高，而非量的顯著增
加。  
合作晶圓廠現階段增產幅度相對受限，預估 4Q26 營收 243.02 億元，
QoQ+9.72% ，稅後淨利 117.49 億元， QoQ+9.20% ，稅後 EPS 39.41 元：  
晶圓代工端產能配置方面，晶豪科 DRAM 主要晶圓代工廠為力積電 (6770 
TT) ，也是力積電記憶體最大的代工客戶。主要代工夥伴力積電因先前將部
分廠房出售予 Micron(MU US) ，現有 12 吋晶圓廠中 P1 與 P2 廠原生產邏輯
代工，在 3Q26 調整至生產記憶體代工，但整體而言，記憶體代工產能釋出
受限，爭取晶圓代工與後段封測產能 (Wafer & Backend) 成為晶豪科當前最
關鍵的營運課題。  
力積電於 07/2026 全面啟動新一波代工報價調漲，其中針對 DRAM 投片價
格大幅調升約 45% 。考量自晶圓投入、前段製造至出貨之生產週期 (Cycle 
time) 約需 5~6 個月，晶豪科配合客戶訂單展開拉貨後，該批高價晶圓成本
將於 4Q26 正式反映於銷貨成本 (COGS) 之中。主要封測代工夥伴南茂 (8150 
TT) 亦自 07/2026 起調漲 20% 之封測費用，受限於約 6 個月之封測與成品週
轉週期，亦遞延至 11/2026 正式認列於營業成本。  
然而，由於市場端面臨華邦電等同業對成熟製程 DDR2 與 DDR3 減供或停
供的結構性缺口，終端訂單滿足率目前僅達 60% 至 70%，晶豪科具備極為強
勁的賣方定價話語權。在 4Q26 產品 ASP 持續走高，且漲幅 10~20% 的帶動
下 ， 晶豪科有能力將晶圓與封測端增加的成本全數轉嫁予客戶端 ， 支撐 4Q26
毛利率穩健維持在 3Q26 之 77.44% 歷史高檔區間。預估晶豪科 4Q26 營收
243.02 億元， QoQ+9.72% ，稅後淨利 117.49 億元， QoQ+9.20% ，稅後 EPS 
39.41 元。  
預估晶豪科 2027 年營收 898.37 億元， YoY+32.77% ，稅後獲利 380.72 億
元， YoY+24.91% ，稅後 EPS 127.72 元：  
晶豪科的產品組合，記憶體佔營收比重最高，約 95%。記憶體當中， DRAM
佔 65% 左右，以 KGD 為主。其他則為 NAND 、 NOR 以及其他
MCP/eMCP/eMMC 等。 DRAM 當中， DDR2 和 DDR3 佔目前 DRAM 出貨量
50% 。  
市場競爭格局方面， AI 伺服器對 HBM 多層晶圓堆疊的強勁需求大幅吸納
三大原廠先進 DRAM 產能，引發傳統記憶體供給端嚴重缺口，缺貨潮由
DDR5 與 DDR4 蔓延至 DDR3 、 DDR2 及 NOR/NAND Flash 。同業大廠如華
邦電基於晶圓廠經濟效益考量 ， 產能優先配置予大容量 DDR4 產品 ， 對DDR2
與 DDR3 採取停供或減供措施，其中華邦電之 DDR2 僅保留供應特定策略
夥伴，大幅限縮市場流通供給。目前晶豪科 DDR2 與 DDR3 合計營收比重
超過 50%，在同業減供退出的趨勢下，晶豪科已成為市場核心主要供應商，
市占地位結構性擴張。  
終端需求方面， AI PC、筆記型電腦與智慧型手機單機搭載記憶體容量提升，
加上實體 AI( 如人形機器人與自駕載具 )逐步落地，帶動非 HBM 記憶體需求
持穩增長。消費性電子端雖因記憶體 ASP 漲幅較大出現部分觀望情緒，惟
晶豪科高階與商務應用拉貨依然暢旺，客戶訂單滿足率受限於產能瓶頸，整

### Page 4

4 群益投顧 
體營運動能無虞。展望 2027 年營運，管理層指出全球傳統記憶體產能供給
增長極為有限，供需失衡格局短期難以扭轉，預期市場供不應求狀態將延續
至 2027 年全年，支撐產品報價結構性維持堅挺， 2027 年整體營運維持樂觀
基調。預估晶豪科 2027 年營收 898.37 億元， YoY+32.77% ，稅後獲利 380.72
億元， YoY+24.91% ，稅後 EPS 127.72 元。  
股利政策方面，管理層指出將經董事會討論定案，過往配發率曾達 50%，預
估未來現金股利發放率將落在 30% 至 50% 區間。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對創意的投資建議維持強力買進，係因:(1)3Q26 營收、毛利
率、營業利益率皆優於預期，(2)4Q26 雖因為 ECB 將送件未給財測，
但客戶對 CPU 需求來看仍持續上修 ，(3)北美車用晶片有望在 2Q27 初
導入 ， 中國車用客戶晶片市佔率持續提升 ， 且持續拓展海外客戶 ，27-
28 年貢獻幅度大幅跳升 ，(4)晶片大廠持續上修 CPU 與加速器的配適
性，更有效率的使用 GPU 之效能，(5)不僅 200 系列晶片，CSP 客戶
的 300 系列晶片創意也有所著墨。綜合以上，上修創意 26 年 EPS 預
估為 63.3 元(原估 54.76 元，+125%YOY)，同步上修 27 年 EPS 預估
為 245.9 元(原估 214.5 元，+288.5%YOY)， 及上修28 年EPS 為 467.7
元(原估 392.6 元，+95.9%YOY，以股本膨脹 3%計算)。考量 27 年下
半年創意的成長動能更加明確上修 ， 且費用控管十分優異 ， 客戶從雲
端 AI 延伸至終端 AI，CPU、加速器、自駕晶片及 CPO 皆有著墨，故
對創意看法更加樂觀，故先前 8600 元目前價到價後，仍維持強力買
進投資建議，目標價調升至 9850 元(27 年 EPS*40XPER OR 28 年
EPS*21XPER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營收大幅優於預期，OPM 更加優化：  
創意 3Q26 營收達到創新高且優於上修後的預期的 187.5 億
元，成長35%QOQ，成長117.7%YOY，其中量產營收達到166.7
億元，成長44%QOQ，推估來自於CPU 的貢獻較大，且在2H26
創意也有新增一些產能支援 ， 且NRE 也達到預估的區間上緣 ，
僅衰退 11%QOQ。 毛利率達到20.59%， 優於研究部預期的19%，
更優於市場預估的 19-20%，推估主要是出貨的晶圓良率更加
提升所致。費用控制非常良好，3Q26 費用僅 13.09 億元，低
於 2Q26 的 13.18 億元，但營收卻成長35%QOQ，故使得OPM 提
升至 13.6%，大幅優於先前預估的11%。稅後淨利達到創新高
的 22.73 億元(原估 16.02 億元)，成長 46.1%QOQ，成長
162.1%YOY，EPS 16.96 元(原估 11.96 元)。

2. 因 ECB 將送件，故無 4Q26 財測，但仍正向看待：  
因為創意的 ECB 即將送件，故此次財報後無法給出財測，但
研究部仍認為 4Q26 創意營運動能將上修，主要係因先前在上
半年所提到的投片保有量外，仍有多爭取的 WAFER，從創意
3Q26 每個月的量產營收就可以觀察到持續增加，單月量產營
收已經提升至 65 億元，預估 4Q26 將能持續穩定攀升。另外
NRE 方面，CSP 下一代 CPU 將預估在 4Q26 TAPE OUT，NRE 預

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

估將翻倍成長 。 綜合以上 ，因為量產營收持續優於預期， 故上
修 4Q26 營收為 244.2 億元(原估 236 億元)，成長30.2%QOQ，
成長 97%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為續創新高的 30.1 億元(原
估 25.35 億元)，成長32.5%QOQ，成長159.6%YOY，EPS 22.46
元(原估 18.92 元)。

3. CPU在Agentic AI世代重要性持續提升： 
根據INTEL的資料中心與人工智慧白皮書顯示，CPU 容量不足
可能造成編排卡頓、工具執行延遲、驗證循環變慢，導致GPU
在等待控制平面 （代理編排） 處理完成時利用率不足 。 若要將
代理型工作負載最佳化，就必須增加 CPU 數量，作為控制與
編排層，在兼顧 新式 AI 工作流程分散且多步驟本質的同
時，將資料高效提供給加速器。故根據INTEL實測顯示，在模
型大小為8B規模時 ， 建議加速器與CPU配比為1.4:1， 模型大小
擴大到70B時，建議配比為1:1。故在AMD簡報也顯示，2025-
2030年CPU產值規模將從260億美元提升至2030年的2200億美
元，CAGR達到50%。創意的CSP客戶所ASIC走到第八代後，CPU
與TPU的配置從先前1:4確實提升至1:2，並且全數從先前的
X86 BASE更改成自家研發的ARM BASE CPU。而CSP客戶第八代
產品放量時間點將落在4Q26末，2027年完整貢獻 ， 故從創意量
產營收看出 ， 因為配比提升CSP客戶對CPU需求持續增加 ， 故上
修CPU26年貢獻創意營收從12億美元上修至13-15億美元。在
27年方面，有鑑於客戶對於加速器及CPU的配飾性相對於第七
代提升一倍，加上下一代CPU將在4Q26 TAPE OUT，有望在2H27
開始貢獻。因為二代產品單價提升，故上修CPU貢獻營收程度
從35億美元上修35-40億美元。 
CSP二代CPU將有望在28年完整貢獻，ASP提升幅度較大，且三
代CPU目前已經開始著手研發 ， 故研究部認為 ，CPU對加速器的
配置開始往1:1走後，創意目前在客戶需求延續性強，且持續
有拿到次世代案件下，28年CPU貢獻幅度將達到50%YOY增長。

圖一、INTEL 資料中心白皮書內容

資料來源：INTEL，統一研究部整理

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

圖二、AMD 對 SERVER CPU 產值的預估

資料來源：AMD，統一研究部整理

車用將成為 27 年開始的新成長支柱 
(1)北美客戶： 
根據供應鏈訪查 ， 北美車用客戶晶片量產時間點將落在2Q26，
創意目前在 5 代晶片進度仍優於競爭對手，故以目前規劃的
投片量來看 ， 研究部維持先前預估貢獻約12 億美金營收不變 。
但根據北美客戶法說，該客戶希望將5 代晶片在 2027 年 4 月
導入機器人使用 ， 故研究部目前僅估算車用的部分 ， 尚未估計
機器人，若該客戶順利量產機器人，則貢獻創意營收有望上
修。 
(2)中國客戶： 
以中國客戶自行揭露的資料來看，全系列征程系列晶片出貨
套數已經高達 1000 萬套，在 26 年 9 月，該客戶的 CEO 在社
群媒體上表示，公司智駕晶片出貨量已達 1500 萬套。另外客
戶給出 26 年征程系列晶片將出貨 500 萬套，27 年將更達到
700 萬套的目標。先前預估 27 年以客戶出貨目標 700 萬套的
50%，350 萬套第 7 代征程晶片做估計，貢獻營收將達到 15-
20 億美元，此預估沒有變化，靜待客戶完成年報公告後再行
調整。

圖三、中國自駕車晶片公司 CEO 微博

資料來源：WEIBO，統一研究部整理

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

兩大車用客戶對創意來說 ，27年僅是開端 ， 首先北美客戶27年
才會導入5代晶片，且是機器人及無人計程車皆會導入，附帶
一提，先前展示的飛天跑車也是搭載5代晶片，故認為在客戶
量產機器人及無人計程車後，晶片需求在 28年將會超過翻倍
成長 。 另外在中國客戶方面， 除了本土市場外 ， 該公司積極拓
展海外市場，7代晶片LIFECYCLE有望比6代更久，28年目前也
以翻倍的狀況預估。

4. CSP客戶三代晶片仍有著墨機會： 
北美另一雲端客戶在10月初表示，200系列加速器已經實現量
產，該客戶已經在各地區DATA CENTER 部屬200系列晶片，而
200系列晶片後段為創意打造，推估LIFE CYCLE出貨量為數十
萬顆 ， 貢獻營收約3-5億美元 。 而在300系列方面 ， 先前市場傳
出主要供應商為美國晶片同業 ， 後來研究部訪查 ， 創意在27年
亦有BOOKING 2nm產品，而300系列晶片由N2P節點打造，故推
估創意亦有部分300系列晶片相關生意，故仍有營收貢獻。而
目前推估400系列晶片有望重回創意，挹注 28年後的營運動
能。

5. 26、27年上修預估，28年持續高度成長： 
創意3Q26營收已經大幅優於預期 ， 加上毛利率擊敗市場預期 ，
另外費用控管得宜 ， 使得3Q26 OPM不減反增 ， 另外4Q26雖然未
給財測，但在產能有所支持加上CPU需求持續推升下，營收也
略有上修，故上修創意26年營收預估為685.2億元(原估648.6
億元)，成長100.7%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為84.8億元(原估
73.28億元)， 成長125%YOY，EPS上修預估為63.3元(原估54.76
元)。 展望27年，CPU貢獻性持續上修 ， 另外也維持車用客戶的
預估值，另外本來就有CSP客戶的200系列加速器開始大幅量
產，300系列目前有所著墨，故上修27年營收預估為2458.5億
元(原估2328億元)，成長258.8%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為
329.5億元(原估287.5億元)， 成長288.5%YOY，EPS上修預估為
245.9元(原估214.5元)。最後，雖然目前28年投片等尚未確
定 ， 不過創意在CSP二代CPU、 中國及北美車用晶片 ， 接續世代
晶片皆有持續獲得新世代案件 ， 另外在CSP ASIC方面 ， 目前有
將從整合封裝能力開始切入 ，CSP 300/400系列晶片皆有斬獲 ，
故研究 部粗估28年創意營收將上修預估為 5145.8億元(原估
4627.5億元)，成長109.3%YOY，稅後淨利預估上修為645.5億
元(原估542億元)，成長95.9%YOY，EPS預估為467.7元(原估
392.6元，以膨脹後3%股本計算)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與

陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非

### Page 1

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr

Document 245: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% Y oY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% Y oY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다.

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
CONSUMER PANEL Q3 2026 
MORE RESILIENCE
3 November 2026
• Average consumer spend across 
the Westpac-DataX Consumer 
Panel remained resilient, rising 
1.5% in Q3 and 4.6%yr. 
• While some of this reflected a price-
driven surge in fuel spend (+0.3%qtr) 
discretionary categories also posted 
robust gains (contributing 0.9ppts 
to the headline rise) with non-fuel 
essential categories contributing 
positively as well (+0.5ptps).
• Households continued to add to savings 
and buffers. Savings rose 1.3%qtr, led 
by younger households and mortgagors, 
with average savings buffers increasing 
to 22.2 months of mortgage repayments. 
• The detail shows financial pressures 
remain uneven but spending patterns 
appear to be converging across 
consumers with low, medium and high 
buffers. Much of this appears to relate 
to a reduction in fuel consumption.

Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel
The Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel is a large 
dataset that gives a timely and detailed picture 
of Australian consumer finances and behaviour. 
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics 
team, the dataset links transaction activity with 
balance sheet information to give a complete view 
of income, spending, saving and borrowing flows. 
All data is de-identified and aggregated to 
ensure privacy. The resulting sample of over 
one million customers gives an accurate 
representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer and is perfectly suited 
to quickly tracing responses to events like 
interest rate moves and energy price shocks.
Powered by 
Fuel and non-fuel spending lift

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Heatmap of monthly spending categories
The Westpac DataX Consumer Panel  shows average
consumer spending remained resilient in Q3, rising 1.5%
despite a further lift in fuel costs. Fuel contributed 0.3ppts
to quarterly growth, but discretionary spending did more

of the heavy lifting, contributing 0.9ppts as recreation
and travel-related spending recovered. Households also
continued to add to their financial buffers: savings up 1.3%
in the quarter and average buffers across households with
a mortgage increasing to 22.2 months of repayments.
Beneath the aggregate picture, lower-buffer households
remain more exposed to higher fuel costs and mortgage
repayments, but their discretionary spending share is
looking increasingly similar to other households.

Spending holds up despite higher fuel costs
Average household spending increased 1.5% in Q3, with much
of the strength again driven by transport-related spending.
Fuel contributed 0.3ppts to quarterly growth, while transport
excluding fuel contributed a further 0.4ppts. Excluding the
direct effects of the price-driven lift in fuel, average spend
was up 1.2%qtr. Importantly, stronger fuel spending did not
come at the expense of discretionary purchases. Discretionary

categories contributed 0.9ppts to quarterly spending growth,
compared with 0.5ppts from non-fuel essentials.
Solid rise in non-fuel spend and financial buffers
Discretionary spending dominates
Recreation and culture contributed 0.3ppts to growth, reflecting
stronger spending on sporting services, while restaurants and
hotels added a further 0.2ppts. Transport spending was also
boosted by a rebound in airline expenditure, which more than
unwound the weakness recorded in Q2 when refunds relating

to cancellations due to conflict in the Middle East weighed on
the category. Discretionary spending rose 0.9%qtr, following a
flat result and a 0.1% rise in the previous two quarters.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Savings growth by mortgage status
Households continue to rebuild savings
Savings increased 1.3% in Q3, with the strongest gains again 
recorded among younger households. Savings balances for 
18–34 year olds increased 2.0%qtr, compared with 0.7% 
for those aged 55 and over. Mortgagors also continued to 
accumulate savings more rapidly, recording a 2.8% increase 
compared with 0.7% among non-mortgagors. Savings growth 
among mortgagors has now outpaced non-mortgagors for 
three consecutive years.
Stronger savings have continued to lift mortgage buffers. 
Average buffers (savings divided by the sum of essential 
expenses and minimum mortgage repayments), increased to 
22.2 months of repayments in September. Looking within the 
unit-record sample, median mortgage buffers jumped from 
6.3 months in August to 6.8 months, its highest level since the 
series began in 2019.

Median mortgage buffers
Fuel spending a clear drag on consumers
Fuel spending as a share of total spending
Spending outcomes continued to vary across age groups. 
The squeeze remains most apparent among younger 
households. Fuel spending rose 5.1%qtr for 18–34 year olds, 
against a 1.5%qtr increase in non-fuel spending. By contrast, 
non-fuel spending increased 2.1%qtr among households aged 
55 and over. 
The gap between mortgagors and non-mortgagors was 
more modest. Spending rose 1.6%qtr among mortgagors 
and 1.9%qtr among those without a mortgage, suggesting 
mortgage status alone masks considerable variation in 
household financial positions – a divide that becomes clearer 
when households are grouped by the size of their mortgage 
buffers (see discussion further on in this note). Note that ‘non 
mortgagors’ covers a diverse range of consumers including 
those that are renting and those that own their homes without 
a mortgage.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Median litres of fuel consumed
More importantly, their consumption appears to be less 
responsive to price movements. During periods of cheaper 
fuel, high-buffer households show a noticeably larger lift in 
litres purchased, consistent with stockpiling when prices are 
favourable. Low-buffer households show much less of this 
response, while their fuel volumes have also adjusted less 
during the recent increase in fuel costs.
The spending data show the consequence. Fuel expenditure 
has risen sharply across all three groups recently, but remains 
highest among low-buffer households, where it also absorbs 
a larger share of total spending. Food spending has also 
declined since 2023. This may partly reflect households 
economising within the category, including switching towards 
cheaper or home-brand products, although further work is 
needed to test this.
Taken together, the households with the smallest savings 
cushions face higher mortgage repayments, spend more 
on fuel and appear to have less scope to adjust their fuel 
consumption when prices change. 
Discretionary spend as a share of total
This suggests that in aggregate, household balance sheets 
are looking healthy. The picture is a little at odds with the very 
weak reads on our Westpac-Melbourne Index Consumer 
Sentiment Index which suggests finances are coming under 
much more pressure. The fact that both average spending and 
saving both rose suggests incomes over the quarter were not 
overly stretched and that more of the weakness in sentiment 
may relate to concerns about the outlook (something that may 
also be encouraging consumers to build buffers now, while they 
still can).
Pressures intense for low-buffer households
Aggregate buffer measures mask sizeable differences across 
mortgagors. We can get a sense of these variations by 
combining mortgagors into three broad groups according to 
buffer size, i.e. low, middle and high buffer groups. This shows 
that households with the smallest buffers are also more 
exposed to both interest rates and fuel costs.

The buffer differences are relatively stark, the median savings 
of the low-buffer group just $2k compared to $15k for the 
middle-buffer group and $95k for the high buffer group. 
Low buffer households face median minimum mortgage 
repayments of around $2,600 a month, compared with $2,400 
for the middle buffer group and $2,200 for the highest-buffer 
group. 
Low buffer households also spend more on fuel, at around 
$170 a month compared with $160 for the middle buffer 
group and $150 for the high buffer group.

We can use average pump prices to estimate what this 
expenditure implies for fuel volumes. Low-buffer households 
consume around 70 litres a month, middle-buffer households 
67 litres a month and the high-buffer group around 62 litres. 
This compares to an average of 81, 80 and 70 respectively 
through 2024-25. 
Median fuel spend by customer

### Page 5

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Yet despite this greater exposure, the gap in broader spending 
behaviour has narrowed. High-buffer households continue 
to spend more on discretionary categories, averaging around 
$1,940 a month compared with $1,740 for low-buffer 
households. However, their discretionary share has been 
broadly stable since 2023, while the shares of low- and 
middle-buffer households have risen by close to a percentage 
point bringing them more in line with the high-buffer group.

Some of the convergence reflects a longer run down-trend in 
fuel spending that has been more pronounced amongst low 
and middle buffer households. This lowering in fuel spend lifts 
the share available for other categories.

Essential spending excluding fuel, meanwhile, has changed 
little across the three cohorts since 2023. The shift in 
spending patterns has largely occurred on the discretionary 
side.

The result is a somewhat mixed picture. Low-buffer 
households remain considerably more exposed to mortgage 
and fuel costs and have far smaller savings cushions. But their 
discretionary spending has nevertheless moved closer to that 
of better-buffered households, suggesting some recovery in 
spending flexibility despite these constraints. 
Conclusion
Aggregate household finances remain resilient: spending and 
savings both increased in Q3 and mortgage buffers continued 
to rebuild. Discretionary spending has also held up despite the 
sharp increase in fuel costs.

But aggregate resilience masks a much more uneven 
distribution of pressure. Low-buffer households 
simultaneously face higher mortgage repayments, greater fuel 
exposure and much smaller savings cushions. While these 
households have so far maintained discretionary spending, 
their capacity to absorb further pressure is clearly more 
limited.
About the Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics service for 
institutional and business banking customers, the dataset links 
transaction activity with balance sheet information to give 
a complete view of income, spending, saving and borrowing 
flows. 
The resulting sample of over one million customers provides 
an accurate representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer. Monthly observations are available back 
to January 2019 with timely updates provided a few weeks 
after the end of each month. As such, it is perfectly suited to 
quickly tracing the impact and responses to events like interest 
rate moves and energy price shocks.

2026 年 10 月 29 日 全5 頁

FOMC 明確さを待つために様子見 
FOMC はインフレ高止まり、景気下振れ、金融面のリスクに直面

経済調査部 主任研究員 矢作 大祐 
ニューヨークリサーチセンター 研究員 藤原 翼

[要約] 
 2026 年10 月のFOMC では、FF 金利の誘導目標レンジを3.75～4.00％に据え置いた。市
場は事前にFF 金利の据え置きを織り込んでおり、決定後の長期金利、株価、ドルにも
大きな変動は見られなかった。 
 声明文では景気判断を上方修正し、ウォーシュFRB 議長も FOMC 後の記者会見で、個人
消費、設備投資、雇用環境の底堅さを強調した。そして、インフレ率は依然高すぎる
ことも引き続き強調し た。他方、金融環境に関するウォーシュ議長の指摘には変化が
見られた。資本配分の選別が進み、一部資産では信用スプレッドが急拡大するなど、
金融環境には引き締まりの兆候が表れているとした。 
 もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は金融環境の引き締まりの 程度を断定するのは時期尚早と
の見解を示した。ウォーシュ議長は今後のデータによって状況がより「明確」になる
と述べ、フォワードガイダンスの提示には慎重な姿勢を維持しつつも、12月のFOMC で
利上げに踏み切る可能性をにじませたと解釈すべきである。 
 9 月時点のFOMC 参加者の金利見通しと直近のFedWatch は、12 月のFOMC での0.25％pt
追加利上げを示唆している。しかし、実施の可否は予断を許さない。注目点は、期待
インフレ率の上振れ、雇用統計、とりわけ労働参加率の改善、社債利回りなどに表れ
る金融ストレスの 3 点である。FOMC は、利上げによる景気・金融環境の悪化と、据え
置きによるインフレ期待の上振れというジレンマに直面しており、政策判断とともに
丁寧な情報発信が求められる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 5

政策金利の据え置きを決定 
2026 年 10 月 27 日・28 日に開催された FOMC（連邦公開市場委員会）では、政策金利である
FF（フェデラルファンド）レートの誘導目標レンジを 3.75～4.00％に据え置いた。なお、今回
の決定について、クックFRB 理事とクリーブランド連銀のハマック総裁が0.25％pt の利上げを
主張して反対票を投じた。 
今回の決定は、市場にとってサプライズとはならなかった。CME が算出する FedWatch（FF 先
物市場から算出される利上げ・利下げ確率）によると、FOMC の開催直前（10 月 26 日）の時点
で、金利据え置きの確率が約 90％となっていた。前回（2026 年 9 月 15 日・16 日）の FOMC で
0.25％pt の利上げが実施された後、FOMC 参加者は根強いインフレを背景に年内の追加利上げを
主張する一方、利上げの時期に関しては慎重に評価していくとの見解を示していたことから、
市場も金利据え置きを織り込んでいた。 
なお、FOMC 後の記者会見において、ウォーシュ FRB 議長は来年以降における FOMC の開催ス
ケジュールに言及した。具体的には、今後も 1 年あたり合計 8 回の会合を行う一方で、そのう
ち 2027 年は 1 回分、2028 年は 2 回分を金融政策の広範な戦略に特化した会合に充てるとし、
金融政策の決定や声明文の公表は行わないと発表した。

声明文・記者会見：金融環境の変化を指摘する一方、慎重に評価を行う意向 
今回公表された声明文を見ると、冒頭で「委員会は10 対2 の賛成多数で以下の声明の公表を
承認した」と前置きした上 で、「委員会は、連邦準備制度の二重の目標（デュアルマンデート）
を支えるため、FF 金利の目標レンジを3.75～4.00％に維持することを決定した。委員会は、銀
行システムにおける潤沢な準備預金を維持する政策を継続する」と決定内容を示した。 
続いて、景気認識については、「経済活動は力強く拡大している。地政学の動向などにより
不確実性は依然として高いものの、国内支出は底堅く推移している。生産性の伸びは力強く、
設備投資も堅調だ」と、前回の「経済活動は堅調に拡大」から「経済活動は力強く拡大」に上
方修正された。雇用環境については、 「雇用者数の増加は労働力人口の増加に追いついており、
失業率はほとんど変化していない」との表現が据え置かれた。ウォーシュ議長は記者会見で、
2026 年上半期の実質GDP 成長率が上方修正され、第3 四半期も力強い成長が見込まれると指摘
した。さらに、個人消費は堅調で、設備投資は、けん引役となっているハイテク関連だけでは
なく、それ以外の項目にも波及が見られると述べた。他方でウォーシュ議長は、多くのFOMC 参
加者は景気が過熱しているとは評価しておらず、その要因として 、自身の見立てでは生産性が
改善していることに言及した。また、雇用環境については、新規失業保険申請件数が低水準で
推移していると指摘し、極めて良好と評価した。 
物価関連の記述を見ると、 「インフレ率は依然として高水準にある。本日の政策措置は、委
員会が掲げる 2％目標への早期回帰を支援するものである。委員会は物価安定を実現する」と

### Page 3

1

3 / 5

の表現から「インフレ率は依然として高水準にある。より多くの情報が集積されることで、委
員会はインフレ率を目標の 2％に速やかに戻すための適切な政策スタンスを決定できるだろう。
委員会は物価安定を実現する」に変更された。ウォーシュ議長は記者会見で、インフレ率は依
然として高すぎると指摘した。さらに、直近数週間においてエネルギー価格やそれに関連する
価格が再上昇していることに警戒感を示した。もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は FRB の取り組みを
評価する指標として期待インフレ率を挙げ、現時点では正しい方向に進んでいるとの認識を示
した。 
また、今回の記者会見では、金融環境についての質問が目立った。ウォーシュ議長は、一部
の企業や家計において金融環境は引き締まりつつあるとの見解を示し、 市場参加者は資本配分
について以前よりも慎重かつ選別的な姿勢をやや強めていると言及した。足元で注目される AI
関連投資の資金調達を含め、金利は幅広い分野に直接・間接の影響を及ぼしているとの認識を
示した。9 月初め以降、市場参加者が投資対象をより選別するようになり、一部の資産では信
用スプレッドが急拡大し、その他の資産でも、金融環境は大幅ではないものの、幾分引き締ま
っていると指摘した。 
もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は、金融環境は引き締まりつつあるものの、現時点で「引き締ま
った状態にある」と判断するのは時期尚早との見方を示した。その上で、今後得られるデータ
によって状況がより「明確（more clarity ）」になると述べた。 この「明確」という表現は、
様々な情報を把握するという目的で様子見を決定した今回のFOMC とは対照的な表現と捉えるべ
きだろう。とりわけ、ウォーシュ議長はフォワードガイダンスを示すことに慎重な姿勢を続け
ていることを踏まえれば、自身のそうした姿勢と矛盾しない範囲で、12 月の FOMC で利上げに
踏み切る姿勢をにじませたといえるのではないか。

FOMC はインフレ高止まり、景気下振れ、金融面のリスクに直面 
今回のFOMC では利上げをせず、様子見となった。市場でも据え置きが想定されていたことか
ら、予想通りの結果といえる。10 年債金利や主要株価指数、ドル指数などにも大きな変動は見
られなかった。 
直近のCPI、PPI が市場予想を上回るなどインフレ懸念が強まる中でも、様子見となった背景
には、景気の下振れリスクへの警戒感があるだろう。ウォーシュ議長は景気を堅調と表現した
が、経済指標を見ると、9 月の雇用統計の雇用者数や小売売上高に減速感が見られるなど、屋
台骨である個人消費には下振れリスクがある。また、けん引役の設備投資も CoreWeave 問題な
どを背景に、債務の持続可能性が警戒されており、 社債利回りは上昇している 1。金融環境全
体に関して、OFR（米財務省金融リサーチ室） の金融ストレス指数に注目すると、足元で上昇
（ストレスの強まり）傾向が見られる（図表 1 左図）。内訳を見ると、ボラティリティ要因がプ

ラスに転じ、クレジット要因のマイナス幅も縮小している。金融環境にストレスが見られつつ
ある中で、FOMC 参加者は様子見姿勢を強めたと考えられる。 
では、先行きの金融政策はどう考えればよいか。9月のFOMC で公表された、FOMC 参加者のFF
金利見通し（中央値）では、2026 年12 月末時点でFF 金利は4.1％となっている。10 月のFOMC
でFF 金利が据え置かれたことから、FOMC 参加者の予想通りであれば、12 月のFOMC での0.25％
pt 追加利上げが想定される。なお、10 月 29 日時点の FedWatch でも 12 月の FOMC では 0.25％
pt の利上げ確率が高いと予想されている。また、ウォーシュ議長が FOMC 後の記者会見で今後
得られるデータによって状況がより明確になると述べたことについて、利上げの可能性をにじ
ませたと解釈すべきとしたのは前述の通りだ。

他方で、実際に 12 月の FOMC で利上げを実施できるか否かは予断を許さない。利上げの可能
性を考える上では、3 つの注目点を取り上げたい。1 つ目の注目点として、インフレ率の高止ま
りリスクに関連して、期待インフレ率に注目したい。ウォーシュ議長はFOMC 後の記者会見で、
現時点で期待インフレ率が正しい方向に向かっていると評価したが、クリーブランド連銀が算
出した期待インフレ率の期間構造を見ると、2026 年 10 月は 2026 年 9 月に比べて上方シフトし
た（図表1 右図）。期待インフレ率の上方シフトがさらに進めば、アンカリング機能が低下して
いるとの懸念から、FOMC が利上げ姿勢を強めると考えられる。なお、11 月分の期待インフレ率
に関しては、11 月10 日に公表が予定されている。 
他方で、2 つ目の注目点として、実体経済の下振れリスクに関連して、11 月6 日や12 月4 日
に公表予定の雇用統計、とりわけ労働参加率が挙げられる 2。労働参加率は 8 月以降上昇に転

じた。労働需給や賃金上昇圧力の緩和だけでなく、消費者の雇用マインドの下支えという意味

FOMC は、根強いインフレ圧力に対して利上げで対処すれば景気・金融環境を悪化させかねず、
反対に据え置けばインフレ期待の上振れを招きかねないというジレンマに直面しているといえ
る。こうした状況では、政策判断そのものに加え、市場の利上げ 期待の過度な変動を招かない
よう、FOMC 参加者の丁寧なコミュニケーションも重要となろう。

Document 344: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

The bank
for a changing
world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.
Article completed on 14 September 2026
Lucie Barette
lucie.barette@bnpparibas.com

Document 373: More signs of life in Hungary’s economy

Source PDF: all report pdf/More signs of life in Hungary’s economy.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Both industry and retail sales expanded in September. Positive
surprises appeared in both sets of data. As Hungary’s growth is
expected to be supported by internal factors, the external
inﬂuence remains the only question mark in the recovery story

Peter Virovacz and Zoltán Homolya
Industry and retail sales surprised to the upside in September. We think both
sectors will contribute positively to economic growth as we head into year-end
The Hungarian Central Statistical Oﬃce (HCSO) has released
September data on retail sales and industrial production. As we
move closer to the end of the year, it is becoming increasingly clear
that both sectors are set to contribute positively to economic
growth, in line with our expectations. Industrial production
maintained its recent growth momentum, reaching a level not seen
in two and a half years. Retail sales also rebounded after August’s
negative surprise, despite elevated fuel prices. Strong consumer
conﬁdence and rising real disposable incomes are expected to keep
consumption on an upward trend in the coming months.

### Page 2

New capacities are pulling up Hungarian
industrial production
8.2% Industrial production (YoY, wda)
ING estimate: 5.7% / Previous: 8.8%
Hungarian industrial performance delivered a signiﬁcant positive
surprise in September. Based on the pattern observed in recent
months, a monthly decline had been expected. Instead, industrial
production volumes increased by 0.9% month-on-month in the
ninth month of the year. Owing to last year’s low base, the
working-day-adjusted year-on-year index jumped to 8.2%. The

positive momentum continued for a third consecutive month,
suggesting that the previous sawtooth pattern may ﬁnally have
been broken. As a result, the ﬁxed-base index relative to the 2021
average moved into positive territory in September for the ﬁrst time
in more than two years, in line with our earlier calls. After more
than three years of decline and recession, this marks an important
milestone in the industrial recovery.

### Page 3

As this is a preliminary data release, HCSO has not yet disclosed
many details. Based on its brief commentary, the now-familiar
pattern of concentrated growth remains in place. With new
automotive production capacities coming online, vehicle
manufacturing performed exceptionally well, as did the
manufacture of computers, electronic and optical products,
supported by rising electronics orders linked to the AI boom. It is
also a positive development that electrical equipment

manufacturing, which carries signiﬁcant weight, has joined the
group of positive contributors. By contrast, the HCSO recorded a
decline in the manufacture of food products, beverages and
tobacco products, which may be related to the negative
performance of agriculture this year.
Looking ahead, the soft indicators also point to the continuation of
the improving trend. The business conﬁdence index remains on an
upward trajectory, with October data showing a further increase

despite higher energy prices. This reinforces the narrative that,
notwithstanding geopolitical risks, the years of stagnation may
now be ﬁrmly behind us. Developments in order books also suggest
that the ramp-up of new manufacturing capacities will continue to
provide meaningful momentum for industry, although growth may
remain concentrated in a limited number of segments.

Turning to the risks: On the upside, the better-than-expected
resilience of the European economy, particularly the recovery in
German industry, could translate into stronger external demand
than currently anticipated. On the downside, oil prices around $100
per barrel, elevated gas prices and supply-chain disruptions
stemming from geopolitical ﬂare-ups in the Strait of Hormuz and
the Red Sea remain key risks.
In the ﬁrst nine months of the year, industrial output has already

increased by 4.0%, suggesting that ING Bank’s full-year forecast of
around 3-4% may need an upward revision. The positive surprise
could also lay the foundations for stable growth in the coming
years, as the industrial turnaround no longer seems to be in
question. The next major test will be whether growth can spread
from a few isolated pockets to the sector as a whole.
Hungarian shopping trolleys are ﬁlling up
again
4.3% Volume of retail sales (YoY, wda)

ING estimate: 3.6% / Previous: 2.4%
After the unpleasant surprise in August, Hungarian retail sales
moved higher in September. Sales volumes rose by 1.1% compared
with the previous month, pushing the calendar-adjusted year-on-
year growth rate sharply higher to 4.3%. Some correction had been
expected following the weak performance of the previous month,
but its magnitude surpassed even the most optimistic
expectations.

Source: HCSO, ING
Looking at the longer-term trend, retail sales volumes in the ninth
month of 2026 were 7.7% above the monthly average recorded in
2021. This marks the strongest performance by Hungarian retail
since the summer of 2022 and, despite monthly ﬂuctuations, the
underlying trend remains upward.
Turning to the details of September’s outstanding performance,
perhaps the biggest surprise was the marked increase in fuel sales.
Sales rose by almost 2% on a monthly basis, which is certainly

surprising given the continued sharp increase in fuel prices.
Sales at grocery stores contracted on a monthly basis, albeit only
marginally. By contrast, sales volumes at non-food retailers
increased signiﬁcantly. Pharmaceutical sales jumped sharply, while
computer shops, furniture and electrical goods stores, and
manufactured goods in non-specialised shops also had a strong
month. While in-store sales posted meaningful growth across
almost every segment, mail-order and online retail performed more

modestly this time. For now, it is diﬃcult to identify a clear
fundamental explanation for the broad-based strengthening in
September. It may therefore partly reﬂect a correction following
August’s weakness, potentially combined with some purchases
that had been postponed during the unusually hot summer
months.

Looking ahead, the foundations for continued growth in retail sales,
and consequently in household consumption, remain favourable.

Consumer conﬁdence remains high by historical standards despite
a recent correction, while relatively moderate inﬂation and strong
nominal wage growth continue to support households’ purchasing
power.
We therefore expect retail sales to remain on an upward trend over
the remainder of the year, albeit with considerable month-to-
month volatility. For 2026 as a whole, we continue to expect growth
of around 4–5%. Moreover, next year’s planned economic policy

measures are speciﬁcally aimed at supporting lower-income
households, which typically have a higher propensity to consume.
This could provide an additional boost to retail activity and
household consumption.

Document 443: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA- SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.

SOME EUROPEAN COUNTRIES HAVE MANAGED TO STAY
COMPETITIVE, BUT GERMANY IS STILL THE EXCEPTION
Electricity prices partly vary due to countries’ electricity mixes
(Chart 3). Electricity-intensive manufacturers in countries where the
share of fossil energy is high face higher costs (Poland, Italy and Ire -
land) (Chart 4). Conversely, countries with a less carbon-intensive mix

(particularly France and Spain in recent times) are less directly ex -
posed to these fluctuations, with costs closer to the United States and
China (Chart 5).
The situation in Germany is paradoxical, as, despite progressively
greening its electricity mix and wholesale electricity prices (averaging
90 EUR/MWh over the 2023–2025 period) and average electricity costs
(across all industrial sectors) in line with the European average, the most
energy-intensive industries face higher costs than the European average

(and even more so compared with international competitors). This is
mainly due to grid costs and a tax burden above EU standards (Chart 6).
9 Eurostat data are used to compare prices with the rest of the EU. However, given that electricity prices are not uniform for businesses at a European level, there may be differences
between Eurostat and national sources. Jäger P ., 2025, “Cautionary tale or model for Europe? How member states should react to Germany’s new electricity subsidy”, Hertie School,

Jacques Delors Centre.
10 P . Devaux, 2026, “European Union: low carbon transition and energy sovereignty, a path fraught with obstacles”
In the first half of 2026, for the most electricity-intensive industries,
the difference compared with the European average was 9%, based on
the prices excluding taxes and levies provided by Eurostat. This gap is
mainly due to the cost of accessing the grid. If all taxes and levies are
included, the difference rises to 21% (Chart 7)9.

EUROPE’S APPROACH OF ADOPTING BOTH STRUCTURAL
PROGRAMMES AND TARGETED SUPPORT MEASURES
European programmes to support the low-carbon transition pursue
objectives relating to climate action (reducing greenhouse gas emis -
sions), sovereignty10 (increasing energy self-sufficiency) and competi -
tiveness (reducing the volatility and costs associated with fossil fuels).
The RePowerEU programme, which was launched in 2022 in response
to the energy crisis, primarily aims to reduce imports of Russian gas

and, more generally, to accelerate the low-carbon transition. Although
Europe has reduced its dependence on imports of Russian fossil fuels,
there has still not been enough progress around the energy transition
and cross-border connectivity. This is due to governance constraints
